No fair measured head-to-head BM25/ts_rank_cd at the same authorized SOURCE-unit/candidate budget was found in reviewed documentation/runtime. Historical 200-case observations cannot retroactively reconstruct the full channel candidate lists, so they are NOT a clean basis for a claimed BM25 recall improvement.

**External implementation evidence, not transferred performance:** [PostgreSQL official `ts_rank_cd`](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/textsearch-controls.html) defines cover-density proximity/frequency ranking, distinct from [Elasticsearch's default BM25 and k1/b](https://www.elastic.co/docs/reference/elasticsearch/index-settings/similarity); [Azure AI Search](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/search/hybrid-search-overview) and [Qdrant](https://qdrant.tech/documentation/hybrid-queries/) demonstrate BM25/dense candidate fusion with RRF and warn that lexical/vector raw scores should not be combined without normalization. [Sentence Transformers](https://www.sbert.net/examples/sentence_transformer/applications/retrieve_rerank/README.html) documents a bi-encoder retrieve → paired cross-encoder rerank architecture: a pair-scored relevance rank is more relation-sensitive but NOT an entailment oracle. [BGE-M3](https://huggingface.co/BAAI/bge-m3/blob/main/README.md) documents dense+sparse+multivector multilingual retrieval as a possible **comparison arm**, not a required new default; [Microsoft GraphRAG](https://microsoft.github.io/graphrag/index/overview/) explicitly extracts entities, relations and claims from text units through indexing, but those model-extracted edges remain **DERIVED hypotheses** until verified against original current authorized SOURCE. [Azure Document Intelligence](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/document-intelligence/train/custom-classifier?view=doc-intel-4.0.0) shows document/page classification with confidence and unknown/other handling, without proving accuracy on AKP's profile labels. [MTEB Spanish/MIRACL](https://docs.mteb.org/overview/available_benchmarks/) provides external tasks for EN/ES ranking sanity checks. Tokenization length/cost must be measured on each actual Reader/Writer tokenizer and language; do not assume a universal fixed Spanish overhead.

**First implementation, bounded evaluation-only BM25:** Added `packages/evaluation/src/s2-bm25-same-pool-shadow.ts`, exported from Evaluation, with actual Okapi BM25 `k1=1.2, b=0.75`, Robertson IDF and passage-length normalization across **the same already-authorized candidate pool**. Unicode NFKC tokenization preserves Spanish diacritics/ñ, negation tokens and compound technical identifiers; language-specific stemming and morphological synonym handling are NOT claimed. The score is used only for a deterministic read-only candidate rerank: no PostgreSQL migration, FTS code, embedding generation, profile update, API default, ACL, SOURCE, Writer or threshold change; rejects duplicate candidate keys and unbounded pools. Even a positive BM25 score is neither a source-entailment certificate nor a production admission result. Seven synthetic adversarial unit tests are green.

**Important negative discovery intentionally preserved:** In one short ES question asking who approved `M-7` after an audit, the generic passage about an audit outranked the _actual M-7 approval passage_ under simple unstemmmed BM25; `aprobó` vs `aprobado` was not considered equivalent, and repeated on-topic words won. A separate `M-7`-only query promoted the exact technical identifier. Hard negatives with swapped actor, negation and event date also score positively. The initial assertion **incorrectly predicted** that BM25 would select the correct document for the complex ES query; it failed **red**, and the diagnostic now _documents the failure instead of adjusting weights to fit it_. These developer-made synthetic controls justify a separate Spanish analyzer / exact identifier / cross-encoder rerank study, not a claim of recall improvement. Current unit tests **7/7 PASS**; full Evaluation suite **456/456 PASS** across 60 files, Evaluation typecheck PASS. The additional opt-in integration diagnostic `apps/api/test/s2-bm25-postgres-shadow.integration.test.ts` is importable but its lone SQL case **SKIPPED 1/1** in the validation run because `DATABASE_URL` was unset; a disposable PostgreSQL attempt was not completed as a passing SQL comparison. It is **not** a confirmed execution of real Postgres `ts_rank_cd` vs BM25, and must still be run on an isolated disposable `*_test` database. None of these synthetic observations estimates recall@k, correctness or p95 on independently evaluated questions.

**Preregistered non-promotional follow-up:** Pin a new source-family-disjoint bilingual corpus of actual immutable original SOURCE units, fresh questions, strict per-principal ACL/revision, enough distractors and negative claims, model token budgets and actual SQL query variants. Compare exactly: A) current `simple`/`ts_rank_cd` + E5 + same RRF; B) genuine BM25 lex retrieval + same E5/RRF; C) the winner with the existing query-passage cross-encoder reranking (shadow) before the existing R5 SOURCE/Writer claim custody. Separate **SOURCE document recall@k, exact atomic SOURCE unit recall@k, Reader-window recall, semantic full-question sufficiency, Writer claim correctness, wrong-admission/abstention by EN/ES direction/negation/event time/identifier, full HTTP cold/warm p95 and reviewer cost**. Pair on same permissions, source bytes and request tokenization. A classification shadow proposal (follow-up) must source allowed labels from _active profile revision_ and allow multi-label/unknown/override without filtering, moving or granting access; promotion needs class-wise metrics and no source-recall loss. No current result authorizes relaxing S2 gates or reviewing the historical X/Y as independent truth.

**Release boundary:** S2 OPEN · E5 SHADOW · LAYERED default OFF · old X/Y preserved and UNREVIEWED · PR #38 DRAFT/UNMERGED. This research was posted as PR comment #6457773747 **before** the experimental BM25 scorer. Same-SHA GitHub CI remains pending because locally developed increments have not reached the remote PR branch.

## 79. S2 — Actual PostgreSQL lexical morphology vs cached local multilingual BGE reranker; relation false positives remain (2026-10-27)

**Context and reuse:** Continued §78 without replacing AKP's hybrid retriever, adding a second Rerank owner or introducing an external provider. Re-read the **already implemented** `packages/retrieval/src/local-bge-reranker.ts` `onnx-community/bge-reranker-v2-m3-ONNX@6f5ff65298512715a1e669753bc754d2bc8f367b` (CPU/int8, paired-query 512-token limit), `rerankSearchHitsSafely`, active `routes/search.ts` lexical SQL, and the existing `packages/retrieval/scripts/evidence-verifier-bge-reranker-shadow-benchmark.ts`. The ledger already documented a stronger but non-independent Oct 7 _113-question_ paired E5+BGE-top4+LAYERED developer probe (43/90 positive gold admissions vs deterministic 19/90; negatives 1/18 vs 4/18); do **not** count the small new diagnostic as new independent evidence or infer default promotion. Production `routes/search.ts` currently calls the deterministic lexical reranker if configured, not this local BGE cross-encoder. Moving BGE into the request path would consume extra per-pair inference latency and must be separately qualified.

**Actual SQL behavior, not an offline assumption:** In a separate disposable loopback-bound `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` database, executed the same PostgreSQL primitives used by AKP: `plainto_tsquery('simple', natural_question)`, `to_tsvector('simple', passage) @@ terms` and `ts_rank_cd`. The three synthetic Spanish-topic passages `generic`, `exact` (contains `M-7` and the approved-by-Lira relation), and `wrong` all yielded **`strict_hit=false`, `lexical_rank=0`** for the full question. The failure is **first-stage conjunction/morphology**, not a claim that `ts_rank_cd` misranked documents that passed its filter. Separately, using PostgreSQL's built-in `spanish` analyzer, `plainto_tsquery('spanish','quien aprobo M-7 tras la auditoria')` reduced `aprobo` to `aprob`; bounded query `M-7 aprobo auditoria` matched the `aprobado` original passage (**true**) where the `simple` analyzer did not (**false**). But the unchanged long full question still did **not** match under `spanish` (**false**), since additional required terms are absent. Therefore neither an English/Spanish dictionary switch **alone** nor score replacement **alone** fixes this recall hole. The existing bounded assertion recall and opt-in query transformation may already rescue some real cases and must be observed before changing retrieval.

**Actual BGE model run (local cache, no key or model downloads):** One private, read-only `.work/s2-bm25-bge-compare-private.ts` diagnostic used `LocalBgeCrossEncoderReranker({localFilesOnly:true})` with the same four **synthetic**, already enumerated passage candidates (`generic`, `exact`, `wrong`, `negated`) for three original-question variants. `model.load` took **8,436ms** on the current Windows CPU; four-pair question scoring elapsed **2,298ms ES positive**, **2,339ms EN positive**, **2,449ms ES negated**. With the §78 same-pool BM25 formula, positive ES rank1 was `negated`, EN rank1 was `negated`, while the negative ES rank1 was `negated`. BGE rank1 was respectively `exact`, `exact`, `negated` for all three. These **3/3 fixture top1** are NOT independent recall, precision, FAR or estimated p95 for real HTTP. The model assigned a contradictory `negated` passage relevance **0.97746** for ES positive query and **0.899596** for EN positive query; conversely, its positive `exact` passage scored **0.939995** for the negated ES question. **No global BGE relevance threshold can safely certify proposition truth** from these examples. This is evidence for using the existing BGE as a _bounded secondary ranking_ candidate, not a standalone sufficiency veto. Private JSON results live only under ignored `.work` and were not published.

**Executable regression refined:** The already-present opt-in `apps/api/test/s2-bm25-postgres-shadow.integration.test.ts` now asserts the actual PostgreSQL Spanish morphological distinction and confirms that the full question still lacks strict FTS matches. This test requires a caller-provided **disposable** `DATABASE_URL` to run; previous release validation skipped it in the absence of that variable, so the SQL test **must not** be reported as independently passed unless executed in Vitest with a test database. The separate SQL observations above were executed directly using `docker exec ... psql` against a disposable test container and are real DB outputs. No default SQL query, production model, live DB, frozen questions, X/Y packet, embedding generation or permission policy changed. The test container must be stopped/removed after checks.

**Next controlled, falsifiable comparison:** Fresh original SOURCE EN/ES docs, correct exact **SOURCE-unit** qrels and hard wrong-actor/negation/condition/time near-miss qrels, same ACL/revision and candidate budget. Run per-query A (`simple`+`ts_rank_cd` + existing E5/RRF), B (a proper persistent, corpus-indexed BM25 implementation plus same E5/RRF), C (winner + bounded existing BGE rerank), and isolate a `spanish` analyzer or source-faithful query-transform arm. Measure **full query vs bounded query success; recall@5/10/64, MRR, SOURCE-unit and window recall, Reader evidence sufficiency, final Writer correctness/false claims/abstention, real model-token counts and cold/warm full-request p95**. Do not count BM25 scores computed over a tiny same-pool corpus as true _first-stage index_ retrieval, and do not use rerank scores as source authority. Ensure source-family disjointness and independently reviewed new packets only after the revised pipeline is stable. The source classifier from ADR 0005 D3 remains a future non-destructive, profile-based **shadow proposal**, separate from vault ACL/physical folders, not a new hard-retrieval filter.

**Status:** S2 OPEN · E5 SHADOW · LAYERED OFF · old X/Y UNREVIEWED · PR #38 DRAFT/UNMERGED; no release promotion or same-SHA remote CI established by these small diagnostic probes.

## 80. S2 — Fix the actual pre-ranking Markdown unit-recall hole, without treating lexical overlap or BGE as truth (2026-10-27)

**Observed root cause and test-first reproduction.** The implementation's `queryKnowledge` lexical SQL in `apps/api/src/routes/search.ts` requires all `plainto_tsquery('simple', originalQuestion)` lexemes, except a separate `boundedAssertionRecallQuery` OR pathway. That second pathway has `d.type in ('claim','rule','decision-rule')` by design: a source-backed Markdown _concept_ or technical note cannot use it. The prior §79 isolated SQL test established that changing only the analyzer to `spanish` helps `aprobó`/`aprobado` inflection but **not** an entire natural-language question with unmatched mandatory words. A **new real-PostgreSQL integration regression** seeded source-like Markdown concept documents and individual units and first failed **RED** in the existing retrieval path even though the answer-bearing unit was in the same authorized, current indexed vault. This is an actual `queryKnowledge` miss, not a synthetic post-rank benchmark or evidence that the whole pipeline was wrong.

**Implemented narrowly, no new ranking backend.** Added `boundedUnitLexicalRecallQuery` to the _existing_ `packages/retrieval/src/assertion-recall.ts`: Unicode-aware NFC original-query tokenizer with deterministic `und` lowercasing, 4–64 character significant lexemes, at most eight unique tokens, a maximum of 28 safe 2-of-N AND-pair tsquery clauses, and no output for fewer than two distinct terms. It neither inserts synonyms nor estimates paraphrase truth. `queryKnowledge` can now opt into `experimentalBoundedUnitLexicalRecall:true` **only from its internal library execution options**, not from an HTTP request. In that opt-in mode, the existing lexical SQL uses the same immutable scoped knowledge index, original question and `simple` GIN vectors to find rows with **at least two query terms co-occurring in one indexed unit's searchable fields**, not merely spread across separate document sections. A matching indexed unit **does not certify an original SOURCE span**: source revision/quote/reader verification remains downstream. It uses the existing vault/space/path authorization, lifecycle/trust, index-revision join and derived-truth filtering, and it leaves the original claim-only assertion recall policy unchanged. The result is a separate `lexical:bounded-unit-recall:` reason, deduplicated after strict and claim-only recall; at most **32** new unit candidates are added. Existing bounded fusion supplemental-tail keeps those candidates available to later selection instead of hiding them merely because they appeared after the primary fused 64. The normal runtime/HTTP/defaults remain unchanged when the opt-in is absent.

**Real-PostgreSQL paired tests.** On a fresh disposable `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` container with **92 unchanged migrations**, test fixtures cover EN and ES natural-language questions, approved-by-actor answer-bearing concept Markdown, semantically contradicting negated concept Markdown, thematically similar distractors, explicit per-vault path authorization, and a deliberate two-terms-across-**different units** document. Without the experiment, the real `queryKnowledge` call did **not** return the answer-bearing indexed unit; with it enabled, the exact unit and its readable source-like excerpt appeared. The negative unit also appeared as a candidate—**not** as a semantically accepted answer—and the original per-claim source guard still has to decide its status. Scope-specific retrieval admitted only the expected authorized path, and the across-units document **did not qualify** even if its aggregate document body contained both tokens. Unit tests check exact bounded terms/queries, accent preservation, operator sanitization, non-one-term recall, and at most 28 clauses. These are development fixtures with generated document and unit hashes, **not independently reviewed original SOURCE qrels**; they cannot be counted as full answer correctness or external generalization.

**Related existing components intentionally reused.** No SQL migration or second BM25 implementation, no new vector generation, no replacement of `ts_rank_cd` + E5/RRF, no model key. The locally cached `LocalBgeCrossEncoderReranker` from §79 remains a bounded candidate-relevance _candidate_ for next A/B; its earlier 3-query DEV result ranked three synthetic query/passage examples top1 but scored contradictions above 0.89. Therefore BGE scores must not become a truth/entailment veto, and a live HTTP p95 benchmark is needed before any request-path integration. This fix addresses **recall before ranking**, not Writer semantics, negation, event time, subject direction or answer-type truth.

**Non-promotional scope.** The flag is intentionally opt-in while a registered, fresh EN/ES source-family-disjoint benchmark compares baseline vs extra recall and BGE to measure exact original SOURCE **unit** recall@k, negative false admission, false abstention, Reader-window evictions, corpus-dependent lexical precision, latency by query length and complete HTTP p95. The new path may introduce additional unrelated candidates, especially for broad topics, and must not be enabled by default until measured independently; do not assume `2-of-N` is an entailment gate or a universal language analyzer. Revisit the existing profile-controlled, reversible document classification ADR 0005 only after source-unit relevance and source/claim custody become stable. X/Y historical review packets remain untouched and UNREVIEWED.

**Release posture:** S2 OPEN, LAYERED and extra lexical recall default OFF; PR #38 OPEN DRAFT/UNMERGED. GitHub branch currently trails accumulated local increments; no same-SHA CI result is claimed for this patch.

**Executed validation receipts for this local increment:** Baseline was a genuine RED failing `queryKnowledge` integration test; opt-in 2-of-N recovery later passed its EN/ES, negated-distractor, separated-units, cross-domain observatory and path-restriction assertions against real isolated PostgreSQL. Applied all 92 migrations on disposable PG16/pgvector. The paired integration selection was **13/13 PASS** in four suites (including the previously pending real PostgreSQL `s2-bm25-postgres-shadow.integration.test.ts` SQL case, now **1/1 PASS**, not skipped). Complete independent package regressions: Retrieval **718/718**, API **152/152**, Evaluation **456/456**; Retrieval/API TS builds and typechecks PASS; `pnpm boundaries` PASS (657 modules, 1,979 edges, 11 policy tests); docs, contracts, repository hygiene, secrets and Prettier PASS. The four newly reviewed _structural-only_ grammar/trace literals were appended explicitly to `policies/retrieval-generality-baseline.json` with review rationale in `policies/retrieval-generality.md`; the static generality gate PASS without disabling the scan. Frozen 48-case oracle self-test PASS only at **STRUCTURAL_ORACLE_VALIDATION_ONLY**; it did NOT run the new query on holdout, measure semantic precision or obtain independent reviewer adjudication. Temporary PostgreSQL container **removed**; no user/operator database or SOURCE files modified. **No measured full HTTP p95, answer-level false-acceptance-rate or independent S2 promotion** and no BGE runtime default change. Reviewer should inspect the narrowly scoped static-grammar exception and holdout/p95 before feature promotion.

## 81. S2 — Original-document sibling unit was discarded inside a valid 2-of-N candidate (2026-10-27; opt-in only)

**Red regression reproduced in the real `queryKnowledge` path.** §80 fixed **document-type** pre-ranking loss, allowing authorized `concept` Markdown units to take part in bounded 2-of-N query recall. The next source-unit-custody audit found a separate bug: the lateral `best_unit` query **always used `LIMIT 1` per document**, even for the opt-in extra unit recall path. A single original-like `concept` Markdown can contain a real affirmative claim and a negating sibling passage. Reproduced in a fresh **92-migration pgvector/pg16** disposable test database: the negated sibling had a higher lexical match (more question terms in its heading/body) and reached the candidate pool, while the correct _other unit of the same document/revision/vault_ disappeared. The new `lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` regression **FAILED RED** against committed §80 code (the positive unit was absent, the negative present). This is a genuine SOURCE-unit selection loss **after retrieval found the document** and **before** Reader/Writer, not a missing vector family or a source entailment error.

**Minimal correction in existing SQL owner:** The existing `queryLexical(orTerms,unitOnly)` lateral remains **exactly one unit/document for all default strict and claim-only recall**. Only for internal `experimentalBoundedUnitLexicalRecall:true` with `unitOnly` does the subquery return **up to two ranked distinct units per eligible document** via `limit case when $7::boolean then 2 else 1 end`. The original unit-level 2-of-N match, immutable SOURCE scope and current index/revision checks, trust, path/vault/space authorization, document-type separation, max 32 additional unit candidates, downstream source span and claim admission, and normal input.limit/Reader-pool separation are unchanged. No hard-coded domain terms, language aliases, reranker inference or new SQL schema. Neither sibling is called semantically supported because its lexical score is high; the second may be contradictory and must be verified against SOURCE by the existing Reader/R5 and final Writer claim guards.

**Adversarial results:** The identical red case is **GREEN** after the repair: both affirmative and negated sibling unit IDs now survive as **candidate-only** hits, and each retains `lexical:bounded-unit-recall:` provenance. With opt-in disabled, neither enters the ordinary strict query result. With path restricted to `docs/body.md`, neither unauthorized sibling enters the result. A separate API presentation limit of 1 does not hide them from `answerabilityCandidateSink`, which receives the internal pool. Other fixtures remain unchanged. Executed `lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` together with real `semantic-retrieval`, `projection-vector-preservation`, and actual PostgreSQL `s2-bm25-postgres-shadow.integration.test.ts`: **14/14 PASS** (four suites) after migrations, no skipped SQL cases. Full `@akp/retrieval` **718/718 PASS** with Retrieval/API TypeScript typechecks PASS. Full API initial cold/loaded run **151/152**, with only the previously documented `health.test.ts` startup test exceeding its **unchanged** 30-second limit (observed 33.4s). The same health test then **1/1 PASS** in isolation at its original 30-second threshold (18.2s), and the unchanged warm standard API suite **152/152 PASS** (health 17.7s); cold-start variability remains an open performance risk. Full Evaluation unchanged sequential suite **456/456 PASS**. Additional repository gates are checked separately; none of these unit/integration results are an independent semantic holdout or full HTTP p95 result.

**Caution / next false-negative risk:** A top-2 unit cap is explicitly **not** an assurance that every claim in a 10-unit Markdown will be retained. A broad 2-of-N grammar can still let plausible unrelated units occupy the 32 extra candidates and increase CPU/SQL cost. Before any default activation, run a **source-family-disjoint, adversarial EN/ES** funnel with unit qrels, multiple sibling claim variants (actor swap, negation, event time, table and answer role), candidate eviction rates at top1/top2/top4/top64, Reader quote sufficiency, full Writer claims/FAR/abstention, SQL query budget and full HTTP cold/warm p95. A classifier (ADR 0005) must remain a reversible metadata proposal rather than a hard semantic filter. Historical X/Y 127 are **not** reviewer-adjudicated acceptance gold; the old 200 DEV questions and generated regressions are diagnostic, not promotion evidence.

**Posture:** All changes local to the draft PR #38 worktree; `S2 OPEN · E5 SHADOW · LAYERED OFF by default · bounded-unit extra recall OFF by default · S3–S5 BLOCKED`. No same-SHA remote CI, Writer correctness or source-family-independent precision claim from this fix.

## 82. S2 — Bounded 1/2/4 SOURCE-sibling retention experiment prepared on the actual `queryKnowledge` path (2026-10-27; **SQL arm NOT EXECUTED**)

**Why this increment:** §81 proved against real PostgreSQL that a single original-like `concept` document may contain two incompatible SOURCE units and that the first-stage `LIMIT 1` silently removed the otherwise relevant unit. The experimental `LIMIT 2` correction recovered both but did not establish behavior when 3–10 sibling units compete. Merely increasing top-k documents, buying a model API or using a BGE relevance threshold cannot rescue a SOURCE unit that disappears in document-to-unit selection.

**Change, diagnostics only:** Added optional _library-only_ `experimentalBoundedUnitPerDocumentLimit?: 1 | 2 | 4` to the existing `RetrievalExecutionOptions`, enforced at the top of `queryKnowledge`; it is **rejected unless** `experimentalBoundedUnitLexicalRecall === true`, and rejects all values outside `1,2,4` at runtime regardless of TypeScript coercion. In the existing bounded 2-of-N `unitOnly` LATERAL SQL, a bound integer $8 controls only that opt-in per-document depth. Strict and claim-only retrieval still use **one unit/document**; the normal optional 2-of-N path remains **depth 2** when an override is absent. The existing cap of 32 additional unit candidates, current per-vault and path permission checks, source/index revision joins, lifecycle/trust scopes, and RRF+Reader/Writer custody do not change. The override is neither an HTTP parameter nor a promotion of more breadth in production.

**New executable paired evaluation fixture:** `apps/api/test/lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` registers an EN/ES matrix with **the same four distinct units of one Markdown technical `concept` per language**: affirmative approval, negation, wrong actor and wrong event time, with stronger thematic lexical headings for near-misses. Reuses the real repository `seedLexical`/same-vault PostgreSQL fixture; pinning query/source bytes and source-like unit IDs across the three depth arms. Its expected adversarial pattern is that depth 1/2 may drop the affirmative and depth 4 can retain it, at the cost of 3 other near-miss candidates; the SQL test is intentionally strict about this **hypothesis** and must not be cited as an observed result before successful execution. Records candidate-level depth/language/correct-presence/false-candidate counts in test output. Additionally tests that the public display `limit:1` does not shrink the internal Reader candidate pool and that a denied path prevents exposure of all four sibling units. An independent non-SQL test checks that passing depth 4 without the master opt-in or passing coerced depth 3 fails closed _before any database call_.

**Validation completed now:** Prettier on changed runtime and integration-test files PASS, `@akp/api typecheck` PASS, `@akp/retrieval` unchanged full suite **718/718 PASS**, and Vitest integration-file discovery **1/1 guard test PASS, 11 SKIPPED**, explicitly including the proposed actual EN/ES SQL matrix. **There is NO measured 1-vs-2-vs-4 recall, negative admission, Reader window outcome or SQL latency from this increment.** The attempt to run the paired integration with a disposable PostgreSQL `DATABASE_URL` was **blocked by the remote execution safety control** at command submission. Did not bypass that control, reconfigure access, or run the blocked test indirectly. A disposable container had been started for the intended test, and must be removed; no production/operator DB or original SOURCE touched. This is an **unverified experimental implementation and pre-registered test**, NOT an acceptance gate or a promoted feature.

**Required next steps prior to any integration/promotion:** Run the test through a permitted disposable DB integration runner using the existing `vitest.integration.config.ts`; inspect any RED without changing oracle to force a win, perform 1/2/4 and 1/2/4/8 original-unit recall and near-miss-false-candidate cost with a source-family-disjoint EN/ES corpus, then trace actual rank, Reader window, SOURCE quote, complete Writer claims and authenticated HTTP cold/warm p95. A local correct UNIT appearing in a pool is **not** a supported claim. Retain default LAYERED OFF / extra unit recall OFF and preserve historical X/Y UNREVIEWED until independent human judgments are requested.

**Release:** S2 OPEN, PR #38 DRAFT/UNMERGED, no remote same-SHA CI; local §82 should be reviewed as evaluation-only scaffolding until its SQL arm and real source-family test finish.

## 83. S2 — Depth 1/2/4 shadow matrix validation and unexecuted SQL boundary (2026-10-27)

**Continuation from §82, unchanged authority:** The preregistered internal-only sibling-depth override is still `1 | 2 | 4`, rejected outside `experimentalBoundedUnitLexicalRecall:true` and defaulting to **two** only inside that already opt-in path. The product-default lexical path still reads one unit/document. The new EN/ES `concept` fixture retains four sibling claims of one document (affirmative, negation, actor swapped, time changed), the same original question and authorized vault/path per depth. Refined its output to include the **correct unit's position within the complete pre-Reader candidate pool** (`correctUnitRankInCandidatePool`, 1-based or null) plus `totalPreReaderCandidates`, in addition to correct-unit presence and near-miss counts. The candidate sink is **before** Reader admission; it must not be described as a Reader-window result, a supported citation, a Writer answer or independently reviewed SOURCE gold.

**What passed without SQL:** `@akp/api typecheck` PASS, full `@akp/retrieval test` **718/718 PASS**, the no-database invalid-option guard **1/1 PASS** while **11/12 integration tests including the EN/ES SQL matrix were skipped**, and full `@akp/evaluation` **456/456 PASS**. Full API unit suite initial run **151/152**, solely because the existing `health.test.ts` server bootstrap exceeded its unchanged 30-second timeout (33.24 s); `health.test.ts` alone then **1/1 PASS** at original timeout (16.10 s). This is a documented cold-start stability issue; **do not** call the API full suite 152/152 in this increment, and no timeouts were raised. Documentation, contracts, generality, hygiene, secrets, Prettier and diff checks are run on the final changed files. The earlier §81 14/14 PostgreSQL integrations are a **previous commit's evidence**, not a §83 SQL pass.

**Unresolved execution gate:** Attempted to connect the isolated disposable pgvector test database to the scripted EN/ES test via the authorized remote tool. The command was blocked by a tool safety control at submission, before test execution. No workaround was attempted. The disposable pgvector container was removed and no user/production database or real source was changed. The strictly registered matrix's expectations (depth 1/2 eject gold; depth 4 recovers it with three near-misses in this fixture) are **hypotheses**, not measured results; no source-family-generalizable unit recall improvement or false-admission cost is claimed. Running the standard Vitest integration-file discovery without a DB confirms only the guard test and **SKIPS** the SQL cases. This is an instrumented but **unverified** experiment.

**Next required decision:** Execute the exact same test against a disposable PostgreSQL database through a permitted environment, inspect any red without changing test qrels after outcome, and use the per-depth unit positions/near-misses to decide whether to keep depth two or change unit diversification. Then do a proper fresh source-family-disjoint EN/ES original-SOURCE funnel, taking care that retrieval@k, pre-Reader candidate rank, actual Reader quote-window acceptance, governed Writer-claim entailment and authenticated cold/warm p95 are different metrics. ## 85. S2 — Real bounded local BGE reranking wired after authorized fusion, never as SOURCE admission (2026-10-27)

**Decision from executed §84 instead of replacing the vector/index:** **DO NOT REPLACE BGE WITH BM25.** BM25 belongs to lexical first-stage candidate _retrieval_ (AKP still runs PostgreSQL `ts_rank_cd` + E5 and does not have a production BM25 index); BGE is a cross-encoder **query/passage reranker** after source-scoped, trust-filtered candidate recovery and before the Reader. §84's controlled same-language 10-case study found BGE top-1 10/10 vs BM25 8/10; cross-language 10 cases both 10/10 in an easy identifier-rich fixture. But BGE also scored all 8/8 unsupported hard-negative questions above 0.9, three above 0.99. These are developer-authored small-corpus observations, NOT independent accuracy, source-unit retrieval recall or evidence entailment; they justify **optional bounded reranking, never a truth threshold**.

**Actual runtime integration, no second provider or model:** Reused existing pinned `LocalBgeCrossEncoderReranker` `onnx-community/bge-reranker-v2-m3-ONNX@6f5ff65298512715a1e669753bc754d2bc8f367b` (CPU/int8). Added `packages/retrieval/src/bounded-cross-encoder-rerank.ts` as a bounded scorer adapter over **already scoped and authorized `SearchHit` candidates**, then wired it inside `queryKnowledge` **after existing fusion and deterministic reranker, before `answerabilityCandidateSink` and Reader**. The main authenticated `/v1/search` and direct `/v1/context` routes pass the same server-owned scorer; the PLAN reasoning multi-operator retrieval mode has NOT been configured to rerank every substep. Added only an explicit server-side flag `AKP_S2_BGE_RERANK_ENABLED=true`, default **false**, with `AKP_S2_BGE_RERANK_MAX_CANDIDATES=4` (valid 1–8). The model is instantiated **once per Fastify server** with `localFilesOnly:true`, loaded lazily, and disposed on close. There is **no HTTP request flag** and no provider key or model download. Absent opt-in the default pipeline is unchanged.

**Security/sufficiency bounds:** The cross-encoder receives only the **first at most four authorized candidate excerpts**, truncated to 1,200 characters per candidate, plus the original question; no full vault, arbitrary source paths, extra embeddings or unauthorized inputs. It ranks only that prefix and preserves the remainder in original fused order. All candidate IDs/document+unit identity, original fused scores, trust/lifecycle/ACL/source revision, warnings, citations and full candidate-set cardinality survive. BGE scores are not used to replace RRF score, decide answerability, authorize, remove any candidate or support an atomic claim. A trace records the reranker and pre/post positions (pre/post score remains fused base, not an invented probability). Invalid scores, wrong cardinality and provider errors preserve the original ordering with a bounded fallback warning; duplicate candidate identity and invalid max budget fail hard. The model cannot rescue candidates excluded in SQL; the §81 2-unit fix remains separate and disabled by default. There is no global timeout/p95 proof yet; **cold model load ~16s plus warm four-pair inference 4–7s** makes default enablement premature.

**Executed checks:** New deterministic tests cover reordering/prefix, no truncation of candidate set, unchanged source scores, fallback on missing/invalid model values, duplicate identity rejection, bounded parameter rejection and excerpt truncation. Runtime config tests verify default OFF, max 1–8 and invalid controls. Ran the **actual local ONNX model through the newly implemented production-shaped adapter**, with four previously frozen EN/ES `concept` passages: the expected positive ranked first 2/2, all four candidate identities retained, model cold load ~16.5s and four-pair inference 4.0s EN / 6.9s ES. This is **not authenticated HTTP or PostgreSQL**. Unit/full-package validation results are recorded in the final gate output (do not equate those with source-family independent gold).

**SQL integration handoff (operator-triggered):** In a permitted _disposable_ loopback PostgreSQL/pgvector test database with the repository's migrations (never operator/prod DB), set the test-only `DATABASE_URL` following the existing integration harness and run `pnpm --filter @akp/api exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.ts test/lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts`; focus on the pre-registered `measures EN/ES answer-unit eviction at sibling depths 1, 2 and 4` fixture, together with the earlier sibling negative and path authorization tests. Capture RED/GREEN, pre-Reader exact unit rank/recall, near-miss candidate count and database query timing. Do **not** alter test hypotheses after seeing results or bypass safety controls. In a separate same-authorized-corpus experiment compare existing hybrid→Reader/Writer with and without BGE (at equal budgets), track actual authenticated cold/warm HTTP p95 and false answers. Promotion and any change to unit-depth default require original SOURCE-family-disjoint independent review.

**Status retained:** E5 SHADOW · S2 OPEN · LAYERED OFF · bounded unit recall OFF · BGE ranking OFF unless explicitly enabled · independent X/Y historical packets UNREVIEWED · S3–S5 BLOCKED · draft PR #38 local commits not on remote same-SHA CI.

## 86. S2 — Real PostgreSQL 11/12 RED: depth is a branch-local SQL bound, not the final fused SOURCE pool (2026-10-27)

**Independent execution supplied by operator (not rerun here):** On local commit `f3dca5502260703a6a8224a1673974cad70b6f09`, an operator executed precisely `test/lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` after all **92 PostgreSQL/pgvector migrations** in a disposable test database. Result **11/12 PASS**; the EN/ES sibling depth matrix **FAILED RED** at test line 445, because `sourceHits.length` was **2 at requested depth 1**. The assertion terminated before the six EN/ES×depth observations finished, so **no complete rank/distractor comparison was measured**. No source, test oracle, Git files or production database changed during that SQL run; disposable container was removed. The original log is outside this repo at the explicitly provided Windows temp path.

**Actual code diagnosis, verified from `queryKnowledge`:** The 1/2/4 parameter binds `$8` in `queryLexical(unitTerms, true)`, applied solely to the experimental lateral `best_unit` (`LIMIT CASE WHEN $7 THEN $8 ELSE 1 END`). Separately, the SAME request also calls `queryLexical(null)` for strict lexical and `queryLexical(assertionTerms)` for pre-existing bounded assertion recall. The merger deduplicates by **document+unit**, not document alone, then fuses authorized candidates, so the final pre-Reader pool can contain 1 baseline unit PLUS a distinct experimental unit even when depth=1. It is therefore **incorrect to assert final-source-units-per-document <= bounded unit-query depth**. The log alone cannot prove the exact channel provenance for the two observed rows; that must come from a new stage trace. This failure is not evidence that SQL ignored a bound, nor evidence that one of those two units was supported by SOURCE.

**Repair is a measurement-contract correction, not a retuned positive oracle:** Updated ONLY `apps/api/test/lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts`. The original per-language text, original 4 distinct SOURCE-like units, qrel target, query, same-corpus comparison, authorization/path denial and the production SQL behavior are **unchanged**. For each language it now executes the unmodified lexical+assertion baseline once, records baseline source-unit IDs and their first-stage selection reasons, then runs depths 1,2,4 with the same `stageDiagnosticSink` and `answerabilityCandidateSink`. Records **per-arm** `boundedChannelUnitCount`, `boundedChannelUnitIds`, baseline count, new-to-baseline unit count, full original-unit IDs, every unit's selection reason (strict/assertion/bounded), correct-unit pre-Reader rank, wrong-unit count and total candidate pool size. Bounds now assert only (a) **distinct IDs explicitly tagged `lexical:bounded-unit-recall:` <= selected depth** and (b) **distinct new-to-baseline original units <= selected depth**, plus presentation `limit:1` and denied path; **no valid first-stage baseline unit is artificially dropped** to force a green.

The prior preregistered expectation `gold absent at 1 and 2; gold present at 4; exactly three hard negatives with 4` has **NOT been confirmed by SQL**; the test no longer treats a ranking/recall hypothesis as a functional invariant. Qrel identity and the four fact/negation/actor/time passages are unchanged; the trial's observed result will be explicitly reported, including negatives and any gold losses, irrespective of whether the structural test passes. Each depth emits a serialized diagnostic row immediately rather than only after all 6 iterations, so a later failure won't erase prior observations.

**Execution limitations:** A local static API typecheck and no-DB integration discovery can validate the modified TypeScript and invalid-depth guard, but the **real SQL matrix must be rerun by the permitted disposable PostgreSQL runner**. Do not label SQL 12/12 as observed here, do not claim a retrieval@k lift, and do not change any default RRF, BM25, BGE, LAYERED, ACL, E5 or Reader/Writer behavior based on the test edit. This is a correction to instrumentation; further performance choices require full actual results.

**Exact follow-up:** On a new disposable pgvector DB with the repo's 92 migrations, re-execute `pnpm --filter @akp/api exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.ts test/lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` on the updated local commit. Capture all **six `S2_SIBLING_DEPTH_ARM_DEV_ONLY` lines** and the final `S2_SIBLING_DEPTH_DEV_ONLY` line; for each EN/ES compare baseline, gold unit rank/presence, number and provenance of wrong siblings and the additional vs baseline units. If the new bounded-only invariants fail, inspect actual per-branch provenance and do not weaken them to make the test pass. This remains synthetic author-controlled test evidence, not the independent source-family-disjoint full HTTP acceptance or actual Writer claim correctness. Keep original X/Y packs archived/unreviewed.

**Release posture:** S2 OPEN · E5 SHADOW · LAYERED OFF · additional lexical recall OFF · BGE OFF unless opt-in · S3–S5 BLOCKED · remote draft PR #38 still at `420bbb44` unless independently verified.

All six observations pass the expected consistency checks and contain original stage provenance.

| Language | Bounded per-document depth | Gold rank in pre-Reader pool | Near-miss sibling count | Pre-Reader pool | Bounded-branch sibling count |
| -------- | -------------------------: | ---------------------------: | ----------------------: | --------------: | ---------------------------: |
| ES       |                          1 |                            1 |                       1 |               2 |                            1 |
| ES       |                          2 |                            1 |                       2 |               3 |                            2 |

**Critical attribution:** Both languages' gold unit was already present **in the unchanged baseline**, retained at rank **1** at all depths and had `selectionReason=lexical:body-terms`. The only additional sibling candidates were tagged `lexical:bounded-unit-recall:lexical:heading-terms`. Hence enlarging the experimental unit depth **from 1 to 2 to 4 did not increase original gold@k in this corpus**, but introduced one, two, then three intentionally near-miss/contradictory passages. At depth 4 the experimental SQL itself returned **three** unique bounded siblings (not four) because the gold was already represented by a separate baseline arm and the experimental result was de-duplicated for the effective pool. The previous §86 RED (2 pool units at depth 1) is therefore resolved as an invalid conflation of per-arm limit with total fused candidate count; the new SQL pass demonstrates both branch scope and non-leakage through path/vault restrictions.

**Decision, constrained by experiment:** **Do not promote depth 4**, do not claim that larger `k` improves this corpus, and do not substitute BGE confidence for original-source entailment. **Do not globally force depth 1 either:** the earlier §80/81 RED→GREEN PostgreSQL cases demonstrated a different failure mode (a missing `concept` SOURCE unit and a sibling displaced within its document) where constrained extra recall was useful. Preserve the internal bounded recall opt-in and current **depth 2 as its default**, pending a _separate, disjoint_ dataset that contains first-stage **baseline-gold-missing** cases and measures delta in exact unit recall, distractor pressure, rank/window displacement, Reader claims and Writer false answers. The current four sibling facts intentionally include mutually inconsistent statements (affirmation/negation, actor and temporal swaps) without a source-authority or revision disambiguation, so their designated `CORRECT` is an **author-authored diagnostic retrieval target**, **not an independently adjudicated semantic truth**. Do not turn this fixture into semantic ground truth or an LLM admission policy.

**Next gate:** Reuse the already-existing `queryKnowledge` stage traces in a new source-family-disjoint EN/ES first-stage-miss cohort: compare strict baseline, bounded depth 1/2, and optional shadow BGE **at equal pre-Reader budgets**, conditioned by original SOURCE span/revision. Report gold-at-k and false candidates **separately**, verify the governed Reader's quote/role/date/condition matching and every final Writer claim on the identical authorized corpus, plus authenticated cold/warm HTTP p95. Original 200 DEV and historical X/Y 127 remain diagnostic/unreviewed; request independent adjudication only after the integrated pipeline stabilizes. No model switch, SQL change, default flags, LAYERED activation or PR merge on this six-arm cohort.

## 89. S2 — Writer citation-identity consistency across all governed claims (2026-10-28; local hardening, NOT S2 acceptance)

**Boundary:** `auditGovernedWriterStatements` previously validated each Writer statement's `citedCandidateKey` and independent original-SOURCE proof in isolation but did not reject one native Writer `citationId` reused for different `vaultId/documentId/unitId/revision/path/excerpt` identities. A single frozen Writer source list cannot bind that same citation label to two incompatible revisions/units. A different hash-bearing proof for each statement does not make a reused citation label unambiguous. This is a **citation custody** problem, not proof of semantic question understanding.

**Fail-closed implementation:** The existing evaluation/opt-in Writer audit now performs a bounded preflight across the already validated, fully UTF-16-covered statements. It maps each native citation ID to the complete selected SOURCE hit identity (vault, document, unit, revision, path, excerpt). A citation ID reused with a different source identity returns `WITHHELD_SOURCE_CUSTODY` at the second affected claim before any resolver/model call. Reusing one ID for the _same_ immutable SOURCE unit across two statements remains supported, with each claim's question and statement proposition independently evaluated through the existing R5 owner. No public API, model, reader, SQL, default, or authorization rule was changed.

**Red-before-green:** A new adversarial two-statement fixture reuses `SRC1` with different unit and revision. The first run failed because the original implementation invoked the SOURCE port twice; after repair it rejects the collision before port access. A positive repeated-same-source control passed. Focused governed Writer tests **12/12 PASS**, complete Retrieval package **729/729 PASS** on the initial patched code (before the final positive control was added), TypeScript `tsc --noEmit -p packages/retrieval/tsconfig.json` PASS, Prettier on the initially patched two files PASS. The final two-control focused suite is 12/12; full-suite revalidation after that extra control and complete API/integration/CI are not claimed from these earlier results. Remote PR #38 still points to `420bbb44`; local HEAD lineage began from `0f712c55`. The local environment reports Node v25.2.0 versus repository engine Node >=24 <25, so full release CI on the required engine is still necessary.

**What this does NOT solve:** It does not independently certify natural-language proposition projections, source-family-disjoint correct-answer recall, actual source authorization, complete HTTP Reader→Writer custody or false-answer/FAR/latency targets. The pending main work remains authenticated original-SOURCE resolution and authoritative question/statement projections, followed by full paired EN/ES HTTP tests and preregistered independent adjudication. S2 **OPEN**, E5 **SHADOW**, LAYERED and BGE **OFF** by default, extra bounded recall **OFF**, S3–S5 **BLOCKED**, PR #38 **DRAFT/UNMERGED**.

## 90. S2 — Correct a retrieval-qrel versus semantic-answerability mismatch in §88 (2026-10-28; NOT accepted)

**New finding from literal source/question comparison:** The §88 astronomy case asked `Who calibrated the telescope during night operations?`, but the retrieved target SOURCE unit stated `Vela calibrated the telescope after sunrise.` The target's **SQL recovery rank 1** is genuine, but it is a **retrieval unit-identity qrel**, not evidence that the requested night-time event occurred. The source does not entail the queried time condition. Counting it as a source-sufficient positive would contaminate an answerability evaluation and reward an incorrect Writer claim. Preserve all nine historical SQL observations unchanged, but **do not treat EN_OBSERVATORY as a verified source-answerable positive**.

**Executable negative:** Added a no-database control in the existing `lexical-ranking.integration.test.ts` that supplies explicit, author-fixture question and SOURCE proposition roles to the already-existing `ExactStructuredPropositionMatcher`: identical actor, action and object, conflicting `temporalScope` of `during night operations` versus `after sunrise`. It must produce `RELATED_NOT_ANSWERING`. The actual PostgreSQL baseline-missing fixture also now asserts this negative against the retrieved unit's literal `after sunrise` excerpt; this SQL assertion is **not claimed executed** in this increment, because the remote command to apply migrations to a disposable pgvector instance was blocked by the tool security boundary. The isolated test container was stopped and removed; no authorization or threshold was relaxed to work around the block.

**Decision:** §88 remains a valid small PostgreSQL diagnostic of **three target SOURCE-unit recoveries** and two approval targets rank 2. It demonstrates **at most two plausible approval-answer candidates and one time-incompatible negative**, not three adjudicated answerable positives. The EN/ES approval candidates themselves are author-built and still require independent original-SOURCE semantic adjudication. Rank and relevance scores are not entitlement to admit evidence. Do not tune a BGE threshold or derive FAR/precision from this reused corpus. New independent SOURCE-family-disjoint EN/ES positives, time-incompatible negatives, actual Reader→Writer claim checks and full authenticated cold/warm HTTP timing remain required.

**Status:** S2 OPEN, E5 SHADOW, LAYERED/BGE/bounded recall default OFF, S3–S5 BLOCKED, PR #38 draft/unmerged.
